# May 1, 2025 PC Packet

<!-- page 1 -->
SOMERSET COUNTY  
PLANNING AND ZONING COMMISSION 
Matthew Webster, Chair 
Jesse Drewer, MPA, DTCS Director 
Jeremy Mason, Vice Chair 
William Cornish, CFM, DTCS Assistant Director 
 
 
11916 Somerset Avenue, Suite 211 • Princess Anne, MD 21853 
Telephone (410) 651-1424 • Fax (410) 651-2597 
Email: dtcs@somersetmd.us 
AGENDA 
 
May 1, 2025 at 5:00pm 
Somerset County Commissioners Meeting Room 
11916 Somerset Avenue – Room 111 
Princess Anne, Maryland 21853 
 
Minutes will be made available to the public via the County’s website upon approval. 
 
 
A. APPROVAL OF MINUTES: April 3, 2025 meeting 
 
B. NEW BUSINESS: 
 
a. Revised Major Subdivision Approval: SA-24-566 Somerset Grove/Clayton 
Homes, Old Princess Anne Road / S. School Road / US Route 13 (Tax Map 23, 
Grid 12, Parcel 45, Lots 1-79) 
 
b. Site Plan Approval: SP25-256 Somerset Grove/Clayton Homes, Old Princess 
Anne Road / S. School Road / US Route 13 (Tax Map 23, Grid 12, Parcel 45, Lots 
1-79) 
 
c. Solar Energy Floating (SEF) Zone Approval: REZ-25-191 Nexamp, Inc., 10960 
Kemps Nursery Road (Tax Map 22, Grid 06, Parcel 284) 
 
d. Critical Area Growth Allocation Application: GA-25-32 Kingsbay Mansion, 
LLC/David Carlin King & Gerald Philpott 27999 Coulbourn Creek Road, (Tax 
Map 57, Grid 08, Parcel 119) 
 
e. Pending update of the Zoning Code to comply with SB931 (Renewable Energy 
Certainty Act) and minor housekeeping language 
i. Special Meeting to hold a Public Hearing on May 22 at 5pm for the text 
amendments to the Somerset County Zoning Code. 
 
C. OTHER BUSINESS 
 
 
D. ADJOURN

<!-- page 2 -->
SOMERSET COUNTY 
PLANNING COMMISSION MEETING MINUTES 
April 3, 2025 
 
 
The following are the minutes of the Somerset County Planning Commission Public Hearing and 
meeting held on April 3, 2025, at 6:00 p.m. in the meeting room of the Somerset County 
Commissioners, Room 111, in the Somerset County Office Complex, 11916 Somerset Avenue, 
Princess Anne, Maryland. 
 
Planning Commissioners Present: 
Somerset County DTCS Staff Present: 
Jeremy Mason 
Jesse Drewer, Director 
Janet McIntyre 
Will Cornish, Assistant Director and Zoning Admin. 
Skip Colborn 
Catherine Skeeter, Planner 
Nelson Brice 
Donna R. Cannon, Administrative Assistant III 
Laverne Stewart 
 
 
Planning Commission Legal Counsel Present: 
 
Andrew Illuminati, Esq. 
 
 
 
In the absence of the Chairman, Vice-Chair Mr. Jeremy Mason opened the meeting at 6:00 p.m. 
 
A. APPROVAL OF MINUTES 
Mr. Nelson Brice noted an error that appeared multiple times in the minutes, his name was 
written as Mr. Nelson, rather than Mr. Brice.  Staff noted the error and assured its correction. 
 
February 6, 2025 Corrected Meeting Minutes: motion to approve by Mr. Skip Colborn and 
seconded by Mrs. Laverne Stewart. 
 
 
B. NEW BUSINESS 
a. Site Plan: SP25-253 Dodson Mini Storage (Jim Dodson), Crisfield Highway/Crisfield-Marion 
Road (Tax Map 64, Grid 12, Parcel 51) 
Mr. Jesse Drewer read a summary of the Staff Report for the record.   
 
“The Department of Technical and Community Services received a submittal from Mr. Chris Custis of 
Chris D. Custis Surveying on February 19, 2024, on behalf of Mr. Jim Dodson. The applicant is seeking 
Site Plan approval for the creation of a new Mini Storage operation, comprised of two phases. The Zoning 
Code requires site plan approval for all new commercial development, per section 8.15b(3). The Site Plan 
was reviewed at a Technical Advisory Committee (TAC) meeting on March 3, 2025. A revised Site Plan 
was submitted on March 20, 2025, and a further revised update was submitted on April 3, 2025, for 
Planning Commission consideration.

<!-- page 3 -->
2 
 
The proposed phased mini storage development plans for the reuse of an existing structure (with a new 
addition attached) as well as the construction of a new building, totaling 88 units. Phase two will include 
three additional new mini storage buildings, totaling 140 more units (phase one + phase two = 228 total 
units). The plan also includes a Lot Reconfiguration to re-combine lots 4A and 5 into the newly named Lot 
4B. There is an existing Forest Conservation Area on the northern portion of the property, the result of a 
1998 subdivision. The property is located at the west quadrant of the intersection of Crisfield Hwy (MD413) 
and Crisfield Marion Rd (MD667). There are two platted, but not utilized, access points to the property off 
of MD667, and a utilized ROW, which is the planned only access for the mini storage, off of MD413. The 
property is ideally zoned as General Commercial.” 
 
Mr. Chris Custis, of CDC Surveying was present to discuss the Site Plan, as was the applicant Mr. 
Jim Dodson.  Mr. Custis and Mr. Dodson gave a rundown of the proposal and timeline. 
 
Mr. Colborn questioned the location of the driveway in relation to the adjacent property line, 
hoping that space could be found to accommodate a buffer of plantings along the driveway.  Mr. 
Custis provided assurance that the area could be utilized as a planting buffer.  Drainage was also 
a concern of Mr. Colborn’s, raising the issue of the capacity of the planned stormwater basin.  Mr. 
Colborn asked about the frontage along 413 and whether planting could be placed here.  Mr. Custis 
and Mr. Dodson described the area as not being ideal for planting for a number of reasons.  The 
turn onto 413 from 667 is already hazardous and the addition of plants would only exacerbate this.  
Also, the area is not far enough away from the proposed gravel drive and plantings would impede 
utilization of the driveway and storage building. 
 
Mr. Colborn asked about the accessibility of the complex for disabled people, suggesting that at 
least one of the end units nearest the provided handicap parking be up to ADA standards.  Mr. 
Dodson agreed with this recommendation. 
 
Mr. Colborn questioned the location of an existing sign along 413, which is not associated with 
the property, but a SHA sign.  The sign was not reflected on the Site Plan.   
 
Mr. Brice made a motion to approve the Dodson Mini Storage Site Plan with staff recommendation 
and the conditions that landscape buffers of 5’ be provided along the access driveway and 667 
property line, any SHA requirements be followed, and that an ADA accessible unit be provided.  
The motion was seconded by Mrs. Stewart and passed unanimously. 
 
 
b. Comprehensive Plan Update: Submission of draft to Maryland Department of Planning to 
begin 60-day review window 
 
Mr. Drewer explained that the full draft of the updated Comprehensive Plan must be forwarded to 
the Maryland Department of Planning for their review, which is a 60-day review period.  Mrs. 
Stewart made a motion to forward the draft Comprehensive Plan to the Maryland Department of 
Planning, seconded by Mr. Colborn and passed unanimously.

<!-- page 4 -->
3 
 
C. OTHER BUSINESS 
Mr. Drewer introduced the Department of Technical and Community Services (Planning and 
Zoning) new Administrative Assistant, Donna R. Cannon, who will be involved in Planning 
Commission communication and coordination. 
 
 
ADJOURN 
On a motion by Mr. Brice, and seconded by Mrs. Stewart, the Planning Commission 
voted to adjourn the April 3, 2025 Planning Commission Meeting. 
 
The Planning Commission adjourned at 6:39 p.m.   
 
Respectfully Submitted, 
 
Catherine Skeeter, Planner

SOMERSET COUNTY 
DEPARTMENT OF TECHNICAL AND COMMUNITY SERVICES 
Jesse Drewer, MPA, Director 
William Cornish, CFM, Assistant Director & Zoning Administrator 
 
SA 24-566 - Page 1 of 4 
 
 
 
STAFF REPORT 
 
 
Meeting of May 1, 2025 
 
Case No.: 
 
SA 24-566 
Applicant: 
 
Bowman Consulting Group, Ltd.  
Property Owner: 
Horace & Elwath 
Location: 
Old Princess Anne Rd / S. School Rd / US Rt. 13 
Tax Map: 
 
Map 23 / Grid 12 / Parcel 45 / Lots 1-79 
Zoning: 
 
Medium Density Residential (R-2) 
Property Size: 
32.47 Acres 
Request: 
 
Revised Major Subdivision Approval 
 
 
A. BACKGROUND:  
The Department of Technical and Community Services received a submittal from Bowman 
Consulting Group, Ltd on March 28, 2025, on behalf of the property owners. The applicant 
is seeking revised major subdivision approval for the extensive modification of the 
previously named and platted Somerset Gardens subdivision.  The newly named Somerset 
Grove is comprised of 79 lots for individual sale. The Subdivision Ordinance requires 
major subdivisions to come before the planning commission for approval, per section III 
C.(1). The Plat and Site Development Plan were reviewed at a Technical Advisory 
Committee (TAC) meeting on April 7, 2025 (preliminary plat was previously presented at 
the September 12, 2024 TAC).  Based on TAC comments the plat was not modified prior 
to Planning Commission, but a further detailed staff review letter was provided to the 
applicant on April 21, 2025 for edits to the subdivision plat. Review comments have been 
acknowledged by the applicant, and edits are pending. 
 
B. PROJECT OVERVIEW:  
The proposed residential development plans for a total of 79 modular homes. The property 
is located between Rt. 13 and Old Princess Anne Rd to the south of Howard H Anderson 
Rd.  There are two proposed entrances to the development, one on Old Princess Rd and the 
other on Howard H Anderson Rd. The property is ideally zoned as R-2.  The last iteration 
of this subdivision was approved in 2005, but development never came to fruition. 
 
C. PROJECTS SPECIFICS: 
a. Access: 2 access points are proposed.   
b. Signage: Signs are proposed but not shown on the subdivision plat.

<!-- page 7 -->
SA 24-566 - Page 2 of 4 
c.
Stormwater Management: This project requires stormwater management plan
approval through the County Engineer.  Drainage and maintenance easements have
been shown on the plat. Sediment and erosion control review needed through the
Soil Conservation Office.
d. Water and Sewer: Sewer and water service are available.
e.
Landscaping: Not reflected entirely in the plat, but landscaping easements are
proposed on all property boundaries.  The plat must be amended to show the one
along Old Princess Anne Rd.
D. DISCUSSION POINTS:
a.
No revised plat was provided following TAC review.  A comment letter outlining
needed plat edits was sent to the applicants on April 21st.  Edits are minimal, mostly
in the form of needed notes on the title sheet. Title change to Somerset Grove must
be confirmed on the plat.
E. RECOMMENDATION/CONDITIONS:
a.
It is recommended that the Planning Commission consider approval of the proposed
revised major subdivision to establish Somerset Grove, with the following
conditions:
•
All required revisions to the plat be confirmed prior to recording.
•
All other pertinent County department approvals be obtained prior to
recording.
•
Additional recommendations as applied by the Planning Commission, be
addressed on the plat.
Attachments 
1. Vicinity Aerial
2. Site Photos
3. Somerset Gardens Plat (dated March 28, 2025)
4. Subdivision Review Letter and Marked up Plat
5. April 7, 2025 TAC Comment Letter

RE: Somerset County Technical Advisory Committee (TAC) Major Subdivision and Site 
Plan Review – Somerset Gardens – Ocean Hwy/S School Rd/Old Princess Anne Rd – Map 
23, Parcel 45 – SA 25-566 
 
 
Dear Mr. Peterson and Mr. Rosebush, 
 
On April 7, 2025 the Somerset County Technical Advisory Committee met to garner comments 
on a proposed adjustment to a major subdivision referenced above.  The following items were 
discussed by members at the meeting, and are summarized below, in no particular order.  Since 
these comments represent a summary of what was discussed, please contact the person listed for 
each item for further information as needed. 
 
Planning Department Comments: 
Contact: Jesse Drewer, jdrewer@somersetmd.us or Catherine Skeeter cskeeter@somersetmd.us 
(410) 651-1424 
• Resubmit updated plat by April 17th to be brought to the Planning Commission for review 
at their May 1st meeting.  
• Provide building plan specs for preliminary review. 
• Provide a landscape buffer along Old Princess Anne Road. 
• Consider coordinating with the Board of Education and Washington High School regarding 
their historic use of the property for parking.

<!-- page 19 -->
Office of the County Engineer Comments (sent via email): 
Contact: John Redden, jredden@somersetmd.us (410) 621-9188 
• Are there any plans to install development signage at the two entrances? If so, they should 
be located within easements or common areas. Ownership and maintenance of these signs 
will be the responsibility of the HOA. 
• All proposed lots should have driveway access to the proposed interior streets to avoid 
potential traffic conflicts on both county roads. A note specifying denied direct access from 
all lots to the two existing county roads must be provided. The lots and streets must comply 
with county standards.  
• The stormwater management facility was designed to manage the two-and-ten-year storm 
events based upon the previous regulations. The current regulation also require ESD may 
be accomplished on the lots. The existing facility is in disrepair and will require clearing 
at the inflow and outflow points as well as maintenance access drive. The design of the 
proposed drainage system and stormwater management must be verified.   
• According to MERLIN, an isolated non-tidal wetland exists near Lots 1 and 2. Approval 
from MDE may be necessary for an exemption or mitigation. 
• Forest conservation approval will be required but may have already been obtained from the 
Planning Department. 
• A landscaped buffer should be provided along the entire site perimeter. This includes Old 
Princess Anne Road and Parcel 186. 
• The public water and sewer service must be approved by the Sanitary District. A fire 
hydrant system may be required.  
• A homeowner’s association will be required to own and maintain any common area such 
as signage, lighting, landscaping, sidewalk and stormwater management facility.  
• Sediment control approval will be required from the Soil Conservation District. 
• Considering the proximity to the existing school, a sidewalk and crosswalk should be 
considered. This should be coordinated with the BOE and Roads Department. 
• The construction plans must be approved by the County Engineer. Easements must be 
adequately sized for necessary maintenance equipment. 
 
 
Somerset County Sanitary District Comments: 
Contact: Richard Padgette rpadgette@somersetmd.us or Josh Taylor jtaylor@somersetmd.us 
(410) 651-3831 
• Sewer and water service available – need to know how connection to the nearest pump 
station is planned. 
• Ensure all pertinent information is represented on plans. 
• Be conscious of and plan for potential flooding of adjacent properties during construction. 
 
 
Soil Conservation District Comments: 
Contact: Mark Carey, markw.carey@maryland.gov (410) 621-9310 
• Sediment and Erosion Control review needed through Soil Conservation Office.  
• Provide a plan for sequence of construction.

<!-- page 20 -->
11916 Somerset Ave., Suite 211 • Princess Anne, MD 21853 
Telephone (410) 651-1424 • Fax (410) 651-2597 
Email: dtcs@somersetmd.us 
 
Somerset County Public Works Department Comments: 
Contact: Woody Barnes, wbarnes@somersetmd.us (410) 651-1930 
• Roads shall be constructed to County Road Standards if intended for acceptance into the 
County Road Inventory. 
• Proposed 20’ drainage easement may be too narrow to allow for adequate future 
maintenance and access – expansion to a 30’ minimum is recommended. 
• Show on a plan where the existing W&S infrastructure to be removed or abandoned in 
place is located. 
 
 
Again, thank you for your attendance at the TAC meeting and the opportunity to provide 
comments.  In addition to the contact persons listed above in this letter, please feel free to reach 
out to the Department of Technical and Community Services (Planning and Zoning) at 410-651-
1424 if you have any questions. 
 
 
Sincerely, 
 
Catherine Skeeter 
 
Catherine Skeeter, Planner

STAFF REPORT 
 
 
Meeting of May 1, 2025 
 
Case No.: 
 
SP 25-256 
Applicant: 
 
Bowman Consulting Group, Ltd.  
Property Owner: 
Horace & Elwath LLC. 
Location: 
 
Old Princess Anne Rd / S. School Rd / US Rt. 13 
Tax Map: 
 
Map 23 / Grid 12 / Parcel 45 / Lots 1-79 
Zoning: 
 
Medium Density Residential (R-2) 
Property Size: 
32.47 Acres 
Request: 
 
Site Plan Approval 
 
 
A. BACKGROUND:  
The Department of Technical and Community Services received a submittal from Bowman 
Consulting Group, Ltd on March 28, 2025, on behalf of the property owners. The applicant 
is seeking site plan approval for the extensive modification of the previously named and 
platted Somerset Gardens subdivision.  The site has been proposed for single family homes 
for several decades, but has never come to fruition. The newly renamed Somerset Grove is 
comprised of 79 lots for individual sale. The Plat and Site Development Plan were reviewed 
at a Technical Advisory Committee (TAC) meeting on April 7, 2025 (preliminary plat was 
previously presented at the September 12, 2024 TAC).   
 
B. PROJECT OVERVIEW:  
The proposed residential development plans for a total of 79 modular homes. There is an 
area to south of the proposed neighborhood that will be used to fulfill the Forest 
Conservation requirements. The property is located between US Rt. 13 and Old Princess 
Anne Road to the south of Howard H Anderson Road.  There are two proposed entrances 
to the development, one on Old Princess Road and the other on Howard H. Anderson Road. 
The property is ideally zoned as R-2. There is an existing stormwater management pond 
on the site dating back to the first proposed development of the site in the early 2000s, 
which will be rehabilitated and upgraded to meet current regulations. 
 
C. PROJECTS SPECIFICS: 
a. Access: 2 access points proposed, one existing entrance located on S. School Road, 
and a new proposed entrance from Old Princess Anne Road. 
b. Signage: Signs are proposed at each entrance. 
c. Stormwater Management: This project requires stormwater management plan 
approval through the County Engineer.

<!-- page 23 -->
SP 25-256 - Page 2 of 4 
 
d. Water and Sewer: Sewer and water service are available. EDU Approval for up to 
79 lots has been given by the Somerset County Sanitary District.   
e. Landscaping: There is a proposed landscaping plan for the development as well as 
a protective easement over the required forest conservation area. A revised 
landscape plan with a buffer along Old Princess Anne Road is recommended.  
 
D. DISCUSSION POINTS:  
a. The shown Forest Conservation Easement has a portion extending over the 20’ 
cemetery access easement. This will need to be adjusted. 
b. A revised landscaping plan to reflect a buffer along Old Princess Anne Road should 
be provided. 
c. Concerns about adequate parking for each lot (2 vehicles per lot), should a parking 
area be added to accommodate overflow and large (RV’s, Boats, etc.) vehicles. 
 
E. RECOMMENDATION/CONDITIONS: 
a. It is recommended that the Planning Commission consider approval of the proposed 
Site Plan, with the following conditions: 
• Revised subdivision plat be recorded prior to site plan signing. 
• Stormwater Management and Sediment and Erosion Control Approval shall 
be obtained. 
• Additional recommendations as applied by the Planning Commission, be 
addressed on the site plan.

RE: Somerset County Technical Advisory Committee (TAC) Major Subdivision and Site 
Plan Review – Somerset Gardens – Ocean Hwy/S School Rd/Old Princess Anne Rd – Map 
23, Parcel 45 – SA 25-566 
 
 
Dear Mr. Peterson and Mr. Rosebush, 
 
On April 7, 2025 the Somerset County Technical Advisory Committee met to garner comments 
on a proposed adjustment to a major subdivision referenced above.  The following items were 
discussed by members at the meeting, and are summarized below, in no particular order.  Since 
these comments represent a summary of what was discussed, please contact the person listed for 
each item for further information as needed. 
 
Planning Department Comments: 
Contact: Jesse Drewer, jdrewer@somersetmd.us or Catherine Skeeter cskeeter@somersetmd.us 
(410) 651-1424 
• Resubmit updated plat by April 17th to be brought to the Planning Commission for review 
at their May 1st meeting.  
• Provide building plan specs for preliminary review. 
• Provide a landscape buffer along Old Princess Anne Road. 
• Consider coordinating with the Board of Education and Washington High School regarding 
their historic use of the property for parking.

<!-- page 71 -->
Office of the County Engineer Comments (sent via email): 
Contact: John Redden, jredden@somersetmd.us (410) 621-9188 
• Are there any plans to install development signage at the two entrances? If so, they should 
be located within easements or common areas. Ownership and maintenance of these signs 
will be the responsibility of the HOA. 
• All proposed lots should have driveway access to the proposed interior streets to avoid 
potential traffic conflicts on both county roads. A note specifying denied direct access from 
all lots to the two existing county roads must be provided. The lots and streets must comply 
with county standards.  
• The stormwater management facility was designed to manage the two-and-ten-year storm 
events based upon the previous regulations. The current regulation also require ESD may 
be accomplished on the lots. The existing facility is in disrepair and will require clearing 
at the inflow and outflow points as well as maintenance access drive. The design of the 
proposed drainage system and stormwater management must be verified.   
• According to MERLIN, an isolated non-tidal wetland exists near Lots 1 and 2. Approval 
from MDE may be necessary for an exemption or mitigation. 
• Forest conservation approval will be required but may have already been obtained from the 
Planning Department. 
• A landscaped buffer should be provided along the entire site perimeter. This includes Old 
Princess Anne Road and Parcel 186. 
• The public water and sewer service must be approved by the Sanitary District. A fire 
hydrant system may be required.  
• A homeowner’s association will be required to own and maintain any common area such 
as signage, lighting, landscaping, sidewalk and stormwater management facility.  
• Sediment control approval will be required from the Soil Conservation District. 
• Considering the proximity to the existing school, a sidewalk and crosswalk should be 
considered. This should be coordinated with the BOE and Roads Department. 
• The construction plans must be approved by the County Engineer. Easements must be 
adequately sized for necessary maintenance equipment. 
 
 
Somerset County Sanitary District Comments: 
Contact: Richard Padgette rpadgette@somersetmd.us or Josh Taylor jtaylor@somersetmd.us 
(410) 651-3831 
• Sewer and water service available – need to know how connection to the nearest pump 
station is planned. 
• Ensure all pertinent information is represented on plans. 
• Be conscious of and plan for potential flooding of adjacent properties during construction. 
 
 
Soil Conservation District Comments: 
Contact: Mark Carey, markw.carey@maryland.gov (410) 621-9310 
• Sediment and Erosion Control review needed through Soil Conservation Office.  
• Provide a plan for sequence of construction.

<!-- page 72 -->
11916 Somerset Ave., Suite 211 • Princess Anne, MD 21853 
Telephone (410) 651-1424 • Fax (410) 651-2597 
Email: dtcs@somersetmd.us 
 
Somerset County Public Works Department Comments: 
Contact: Woody Barnes, wbarnes@somersetmd.us (410) 651-1930 
• Roads shall be constructed to County Road Standards if intended for acceptance into the 
County Road Inventory. 
• Proposed 20’ drainage easement may be too narrow to allow for adequate future 
maintenance and access – expansion to a 30’ minimum is recommended. 
• Show on a plan where the existing W&S infrastructure to be removed or abandoned in 
place is located. 
 
 
Again, thank you for your attendance at the TAC meeting and the opportunity to provide 
comments.  In addition to the contact persons listed above in this letter, please feel free to reach 
out to the Department of Technical and Community Services (Planning and Zoning) at 410-651-
1424 if you have any questions. 
 
 
Sincerely, 
 
Catherine Skeeter 
 
Catherine Skeeter, Planner

SOMERSET COUNTY 
DEPARTMENT OF TECHNICAL AND COMMUNITY SERVICES 
Jesse Drewer, MPA, Director 
William Cornish, CFM, Assistant Director & Zoning Administrator 
 
REZ 25-191 - Page 1 of 4 
 
 
 
STAFF REPORT 
 
 
Meeting of May 1, 2025 
 
Case No.: 
 
REZ 25-191 
Applicant: 
 
Nexamp, Inc.  
Property Owner: 
Kathleen E. Keller 
Location: 
10960 Kemps Nursery Road 
Tax Map: 
 
Map 22 / Grid 6 / Parcel 284 
Zoning: 
 
Agricultural-Residential (AR) 
Property Size: 
136.86 Acres 
Request: 
 
Utility Scale Solar Energy Facility Floating Zone Approval 
 
 
A. BACKGROUND:  
 
The Department of Technical and Community Services received an application from 
Bohler Engineering on November 26, 2024, on behalf of the applicant, Nexamp Inc. The 
applicant is seeking Utility Scale Solar Energy Facility (SEF) Floating Zone Approval for 
property located at 10960 Kemps Nursery Road. Following the procedure set forth in the 
Somerset County Zoning Code section 7.7, an application is submitted to the Board of 
County Commissioners who, following a vote, refer the application to the Planning 
Commission. The request was initially heard by the County Commissioners on December 
3rd where it was denied. Following discussions over the next month with staff, the matter 
was brought back up to the County Commissioners for a referral recommendation on 
January 28, 2025, where it was forwarded to the Planning Commission for a public hearing 
and discussion. Following the public hearing on March 6, 2025, a motion was made and 
approved to table the request until a future meeting so an additional survey and site plan 
could be prepared based on the discussion from the March 6th meeting. 
 
The Somerset County Zoning ordinance requires all solar energy facilities generating more 
than two (2) megawatts in electricity go through the SEF Floating Zone process. Pending 
a recommendation by the Planning Commission, a public hearing will be held by the 
County Commissioners on the request. If the floating zone is granted, the applicant would 
then be permitted to submit for the standard site plan review process by staff and the 
Planning Commission.

<!-- page 75 -->
REZ 25-191 - Page 2 of 4 
 
B. PROJECT OVERVIEW:  
 
The applicant proposes a solar energy facility that generates 5 megawatts of electricity. It 
is proposed to be 3 independent systems with 1 1MW and 2 2MW facilities. The proposed 
solar energy facility will be located on approximately 30 of the 136 acres that comprise the 
site. Located approximately 0.4 miles down Kemps Nursery Road, the property and 
surrounding area are primarily agricultural in nature. The property is zoned as Agricultural-
Residential and complies with the conditions outlined in section 7.5c of the zoning code 
[Staff Exhibit #1] which states; 
 
I. 
Utility scale solar energy facilities will only be considered on parcels 
designated as ‘Agricultural Residential’ (AR) or ‘General Industrial’ (I-2). 
II. 
Siting of utility scale solar energy facilities will not be considered for 
placement within 1,500 feet of the right-of-way for U.S. Route 13, Ocean 
Highway or M.D. Route 413, Crisfield Highway. 
III. 
Utility scale solar energy facilities may not be considered for parcels 
consisting of 50% or more prime agricultural soils as determined by the 
Natural Resources Conservation Service Soil Service Soil Survey as Class 
I, II, or III, and as further advised by the Somerset County Soil Conservation 
District. 
IV. 
Development Cap. No more than 1,800 acres of utility scale solar will be 
considered within the County as defined by the limits of development for 
each utility scale solar energy facility developed or proposed. 
 
 
It should be noted that the above condition III, after consulting with legal counsel, is not 
explicitly met, as the site contains greater than 50% prime agricultural soils, by rough staff 
calculation through Web Soil Survey, the approximate Class I-III soils is 73.6%. [Staff 
Exhibit #2] 
 
 
Section 7.5(A), which is the statement of intent for the SEF states, “it is intended that utility 
scale solar energy facilities are not placed on prime agricultural lands, are aesthetically 
attractive, are placed so as to protect the commercial viability of the U.S. Route 13 and 
M.D. Route 413 corridors, and are compatible with the surrounding neighborhood.” The 
Commission must determine whether, in light of the soils analysis, a recommendation for 
an SEF is appropriate based on the conceptual site plan. [Applicant Exhibit #2]

<!-- page 76 -->
REZ 25-191 - Page 3 of 4 
 
 
C. PROJECTS SPECIFICS: 
Zoning Code Requirements: 
To be granted Floating Zone Approval, a narrative addressing the following criteria must 
be addressed; 
I. 
Statement of present and proposed ownership of all land within the development; 
II. 
Overall objectives of the proposed development plan and a statement of how the 
proposed development concept corresponds to and complies with the purpose of 
the district; 
III. 
The method of providing sewer and water service and other utilities, such as, but 
not limited to: telephone, gas, and electric services, if applicable. 
IV. 
The method of and responsibility for maintenance of open areas, private streets, 
recreational amenities, and parking areas; 
V. 
A general description of architectural and landscape elements, including graphic 
representations; and 
VI. 
If the applicant desires to develop the property in phases, a preliminary phasing 
plan indicating 
a. The phase(s) in which the project will be developed, including the 
approximate land area, uses, densities, and public facilities to be developed 
during each phase. 
b. If different land use types are to be included within the development plan, 
the plan must include the mix of uses anticipated to be built in each phase. 
 
 
All six (6) items are addressed to staff satisfaction and code compliance in the narratives dated 
February 4, 2025 and February 24, 2025. [Staff Exhibit #3] 
 
Floodplain and Critical Area: 
Due to the new location of the solar array on the property, it will now be located within a floodplain 
and within the Critical Area Resource Conservation Area.  
All electrical equipment located within the floodplain will be required to be elevated above base 
flood elevation (BFE). 
Following approval of the SEF Zone and prior to the submission of a site plan, compliance with 
COMAR 27.01.14 (Renewable Energy Generating System within the Chesapeake Bay Critical 
Area) must be met.

<!-- page 77 -->
REZ 25-191 - Page 4 of 4 
 
D. REQUIREMENTS FOR THE PUBLIC HEARING:  
 
Section 7.7b of the Zoning Code outlines the procedure for the public hearing, which 
states it must be posted in the newspaper, property posted, and certified mail letters 
sent to adjacent property owners. All of these requirements have been met with the 
public hearing held on March 6th. [Staff Exhibit #4] 
 
E. DISCUSSION ITEMS: 
 
The following are recommended discussion items from staff and the applicant that the 
Planning Commission should review and consider prior to making a decision. 
 
a. Additional landscaping around the full perimeter of the solar array. 
b. Compliance with Floodplain and Critical Area requirements. 
c. Request for construction timeline extension from one (1) year to three (3) years. 
d. Request for ground cover vegetation height to be extended to 36 inches from 
12 inches 
e. Request for landscape planting to be completed before construction is finalized 
rather than at the beginning of construction. 
 
F. STAFF RECOMMENDATION AND CONDITIONS OF APPROVAL: 
 
a. It is recommended that the Planning Commission consider approval of the proposed 
Utility Scale Solar Energy Facility (SEF) Floating Zone, with the following 
conditions: 
• All conditions imposed by the Planning Commission be met. 
• If the Floating Zone is approved by the County Commissioners, the full site 
plan is to be reviewed by the Technical Advisory Committee and Planning 
Commission prior to approval.

Section 7. Overlay and Floating Districts 
Somerset County Zoning Ordinance – February 2024 
7-12 
 
7.5  Utility Scale Solar Energy Facility Floating Zone (SEF) 
a. Statement of Intent.  
The purpose of the utility scale solar floating zone is to allow for the orderly development of 
utility scale solar energy facilities typically generating more than two (2) megawatts in 
electricity (AC) that are appropriately sited and sized. Furthermore, it is intended that utility 
scale solar energy facilities are not placed on prime agricultural lands, are aesthetically 
attractive, are placed so as to protect the commercial viability of the U.S. Route 13 and M.D. 
Route 413 corridors, and are compatible with the surrounding neighborhood.   
b. Application for Floating Zone. 
Refer to Section 7.7 ‘Procedure for Floating Zone District Approval.’  
c. Applicability. 
(1) Utility scale solar energy facilities will only be considered on parcels designated as 
‘Agricultural Residential’ (AR) or ‘General Industrial’ (I-2).  
(2) Siting of utility scale solar energy facilities will not be considered for placement within 
1,500 feet of the right-of-way for U.S. Route 13, Ocean Highway or M.D. Route 413, 
Crisfield Highway.  
(3) Utility scale solar energy facilities may not be considered for parcels consisting of 50% 
or more prime agricultural soils as determined by the Natural Resources Conservation 
Service Soil Service Soil Survey as Class I, II, or III, and as further advised by the 
Somerset County Soil Conservation District.  
(4) Development Cap. No more than 1,800 acres of utility scale solar will be considered 
within the County as defined by the limits of development for each utility scale solar 
energy facility developed or proposed.  
d. Site Plan Requirements. 
(1) Site Plans must be submitted in accordance with Section 8.15  
(2) Additional site plan information required must include, but is not limited to the 
following: 
i. 
Total acreage of the parcel(s), acreage on the parcel(s) within the limits of 
disturbance, acreage under the solar energy facility equipment including panels, 
inverters, transformers, and wiring; 
ii. 
The amount of energy in megawatts (MW) that the facility is expected to generate 
in alternating current (AC) and direct current (DC); 
iii. Dimensions, locations, and orientation of the solar energy equipment. The site 
plan must also show the type of system proposed (i.e. tracking or stationary); 
iv. The number of posts or pilings anticipated to be placed on the parcel(s);  
v. 
The materials that are anticipated to be utilized (i.e. aluminum, copper, etc.) and 
approximate percentages; 
vi. Location of interconnection to any existing substations. In the event that a new 
substation or connector station is proposed, it must also be shown on the site plan; 
vii. Location of staging areas for construction including details describing the 
materials to be used as well as the location of any permanent structures for the 
long term maintenance of the facility or offices; 
viii. The location of internal access roads and materials to be used; 
ix. Existing and proposed drainage facilities on the parcel(s); 
x. 
Distances to dwellings on adjacent properties; 
xi. Site distance triangles, if applicable; 
xii. Location of fencing, if applicable;

<!-- page 79 -->
Section 7. Overlay and Floating Districts 
Somerset County Zoning Ordinance – February 2024 
7-13 
 
xiii. If applicable, a landscape plan that addresses maintenance of any dead or dying 
vegetation; 
xiv. Any additional information requested by the Zoning Administrator.  
e. Additional Considerations.  
(1) Solar Energy Facilities as a principal use generating less than two (2) Megawatts (MW) 
of electricity may be eligible for full or partial exemption from sections 7.5(c), 7.5(e)(2)(i 
–iii), 7.5(i)(1-5), and 7.5(j)(7) if determined to be appropriate by the Planning 
Commission.  
i. Furthermore, in order for a solar energy facility generating less than two (2) MW 
to be eligible for exemption under section 7.5(e)(1) the facility is not permitted 
to be located on the same parcel as an existing or proposed solar energy facility. 
ii.   Solar energy facilities generating less than two (2) MW may provide an 
irrevocable letter of credit to meet the financial surety requirements for 
decommissioning and the long term maintenance of landscaping and fencing, if 
approved by the County Commissioners. 
(2) A narrative of the proposed utility scale solar energy system must accompany the 
application and site plan. The narrative must include the following, but is not limited to: 
i. 
Upgrades or improvements to the current electric grid that are required to support 
the proposed solar energy facility; 
ii. 
The determination or status of the applicant(s) intent to pursue interconnection to 
the grid and the status of the application for a ‘Certificate of Public Convenience 
and Necessity’ (CPCN) with the Maryland Public Service Commission (PSC); 
iii. A plan for the long term maintenance of the facility is required. Innovative 
solutions are encouraged. The long term maintenance plan must address the 
following: 
(a) Solar 
energy 
equipment 
including 
panels, 
racking, 
inverters, 
transformers, substations, or connector stations (if applicable), and 
wiring; 
(b) Drainage; 
(c) Turf, along with any anticipated herbicide and pesticide usage; 
(d) Other landscaping, including a plan for the maintenance of dead or dying 
vegetation; 
(e) Invasive species management; 
(f) Natural habitat protection and mitigation measures, if applicable; 
(g) Fencing, if applicable; 
(h) Glare;  
(i) Noise and dust control during construction.  
A maintenance plan is required and must be approved by the County as part of 
an application for a building permit. The maintenance plan must be accompanied 
by an appropriate surety in the form of cash, or a bond in an amount equal to 
100% of the cost of maintenance for landscaping including turf and fencing plus 
a ten (10%) contingency for a period of five (5) years. This amount may be 
reduced to fifteen (15%) percent of the landscaping and fence maintenance 
required for a period of thirty (30) years based on an estimate prepared by a 
Maryland licensed engineer provided that the Department of Technical and 
Community Services has determined the survivability of vegetation and that any 
other issues identified have been resolved. The landscaping and fencing

<!-- page 80 -->
Section 7. Overlay and Floating Districts 
Somerset County Zoning Ordinance – February 2024 
7-14 
 
maintenance cost estimate must be reevaluated every ten (10) years by a 
Maryland licensed engineer. In the event that the solar energy facility continues 
operation in any capacity after a period of thirty (30) years, another maintenance 
plan must be submitted and approved by the County with an accompanying 
financial surety as provided above.  
iv. A construction plan including, but not limited to, hours of operation, duration of 
construction, number of trucks entering and leaving the site, lighting, anticipated 
noise generated, etc.  
v. 
A drainage plan is required as part of stormwater management in conformance 
with current Maryland regulations.   
vi. The applicants must provide a plan for any remaining portion of any parcel that 
is not designated to be developed for the solar energy system. This may include 
the continuance of farming on a portion of a parcel, pollinator friendly habitats, 
or others.  
(3) All applicable local, state, and federal regulations must be adhered to. This may include, 
but is not limited to: the Chesapeake Bay Critical Area Ordinance, Forest Conservation 
Ordinance, erosion and sediment control, stormwater regulations, and the Floodplain 
Ordinance.  
(4) Construction must begin within one (1) year of approval by the County Commissioners. 
Extensions may be granted by the County Commissioners, however in no event shall the 
extension surpass a total period of three (3) years.  
(5) Future Technology: In the event that the solar energy facility proposes to change its 
footprint, height, or any other modification that materially affects the impacts created by 
the facility at the time of installation due to technological advances after initial 
installation, county approval is required.  
(6) In the event that it is determined by the Maryland State Health Department or any other 
applicable government agency that the solar energy facility is comprised of hazardous 
materials at any point after installation, the owner or assigns is jointly and severally liable 
for any mitigation required. 
(7) Furthermore, if on-site storage of electricity is proposed for or after initial installation, a 
plan for maintenance and emergencies must be submitted to the County before placement 
on the parcel(s).  
(8) Panel supports or solar array posts are not allowed to be placed in drainage ditches. A 
fifteen (15) foot wide maintenance area must be provided adjacent to ditches.   
(9) Collector-style solar energy facilities that utilize mirrors are not permitted.  
(10) All wiring must be underground except where necessary to connect to the grid.  
(11) Topsoil is not permitted to be removed from the property(s).  
(12) Materials and solvents used for cleaning solar energy equipment must be biodegradable.  
(13) Any required right-of-way(s) must be identified and secured through an easement, lease, 
service agreement or other legally binding document prior to site plan approval.   
(14) In the event that the Maryland Public Service Commission’s (PSC) Certificate of Public 
Convenience and Necessity (CPCN) conditions are more stringent than those required 
by the County, the stricter of the two (2) must apply.  
f. Financial Surety. 
(1) Financial surety will be required for decommissioning and the long term maintenance of 
the landscaping and fencing in the form of cash, or a bond. A bond for the repair of roads

<!-- page 81 -->
Section 7. Overlay and Floating Districts 
Somerset County Zoning Ordinance – February 2024 
7-15 
 
may be required as determined either by the Maryland State Highway Administration or 
Somerset County Department of Public Works Roads and Waterways Division.  
g. Additional Notification Requirements.  
(1) A single point of contact must be provided to the Department of Technical and 
Community Services for any change in solar energy facility ownership in order to 
address complaints and must be updated annually.  
(2) A public informational meeting must be held by the applicant(s) separate from Somerset 
County required meetings and hearings for all interested parties including adjacent 
property owners to explain the project and discuss potential impacts. The informational 
meeting must be held prior to the first meeting of the Planning Commission on the 
application.  
(3) Local fire departments and emergency services personnel must receive notice of the 
proposed facility by the applicants. In the event that training is desired by these persons, 
the solar energy facility owner or its assigns must provide this training within three (3) 
months of commercial operation.  
(4) Notification letters must be sent by the applicant via first-class mail to all property 
owners within five-hundred (500) feet of the property(s). A copy of the notification letter 
must be provided to the Department of Technical and Community Services. A 
notification letter must be sent at least fifteen (15) days prior to the public hearing with 
the Planning Commission and again prior to the public hearing with the County 
Commissioners. At a minimum, the notification letter must contain the following: 
i. 
A description of the project proposal including the number of acreage and the 
generating capacity of the facility; 
ii. 
The name of the project, the project owner, and the property owner; 
iii. The time, date, and location of the public hearing;  
iv. A means to contact the project owner.  
h. Bulk Regulations.  
(1) Utility scale solar energy facilities must be setback seventy-five (75) feet from adjacent 
property lines. Setback requirements may be waived for an adjacent parcel which is 
intended to be developed with utility scale solar and is included in the same application 
package.  
(2) New substations or connector stations, inverters, and transformers must be setback six-
hundred (600) feet or more from existing dwellings, schools, churches, public right-of-
ways, and subdivisions of ten (10) or more improved lots.  
(3) All components of a utility scale solar energy facility including underground wiring must 
be setback at least one-hundred (100) feet from a cemetery or graveyard.

<!-- page 82 -->
Section 7. Overlay and Floating Districts 
Somerset County Zoning Ordinance – February 2024 
7-16 
 
i. Visual Standards.  
(1) Visual Shield.  
i. 
A full visual shield must be provided on all sides of the solar energy facility 
including areas around any new substation or connector stations. A combination 
of vegetation, fencing, berms, or other materials may be used in accomplishing 
this standard. If vegetation is utilized to meet this standard, the visual shield must 
be one-hundred (100%) percent opaque within five (5) years from the start of 
construction, planted at six (6) feet in height at installation, and must reach a 
height of at least eight (8) feet. Existing on-site vegetation, fencing, berms, or 
other materials that effectively serve as a visual shield may be considered to meet 
this standard. Creativity is encouraged.  
(2) Visual Impact of Appurtenances. 
i. 
Furthermore, it is intended that inverters, transformers, and any other 
appurtenances associated with the solar panel arrays visually blend with the 
adjacent parcel(s) and be as unobtrusive as possible. This may include naturally 
colored casing (green, brown, etc.) and/or a vegetative buffer.  
(3) Landscaping, Turf. 
i. 
Low growth grass consisting of native and/or salt tolerant species is required. 
Pollinator friendly habitat or areas for animal husbandry may be created, however 
the grounds surrounding and within the facility must be kept in a reasonable state 
and height at all times. The uncontrolled growth of weeds, grass, or other similar 
vegetation is not permitted to exceed twelve (12) inches in height on any lot or 
parcel developed with a utility scale solar energy facility.  
(4) Landscaping, Plantings. 
i. 
If landscaping is proposed to meet the visual shield standard, solar energy facility 
owners are encouraged to plant at the beginning of construction. Care should be 
given to the timing and placement of vegetation on-site.   
ii. 
No more than 50% non-native species may be used.  
iii. At least three (3) rows of evergreen plant materials including trees and shrubs 
reaching a height of at least eight (8) feet within five (5) years and planted at six 
(6) feet in height at installation, is required if vegetation is used to meet the visual 
shield standard.3 
iv. Vegetation existing on the parcel(s) may be used to meet the visual buffer 
requirements if it is determined by the Department of Technical and Community 
Services that it is sufficient to meet the requirement. The vegetation to be retained 
for this purpose must be shown on the site plan.  
(5) Fencing.  
i. 
Fencing is not required but may be necessary for security. If fencing is used, the 
applicant(s) is encouraged to utilize alternative and innovative means of facility 
protection and visual shielding. This could include berms and/or agriculturally 
friendly fencing. Fences may not exceed eight (8) feet in height.  
(6) Glare. 
i. 
In the event that glare is identified by the Department of Technical and 
Community Services, the owner of the solar energy facility, or its assigns, must 
address the concerns within two (2) months of identification. If new vegetation 
                                                 
3 As Amended and Adopted in Ordinance 1174

<!-- page 83 -->
Section 7. Overlay and Floating Districts 
Somerset County Zoning Ordinance – February 2024 
7-17 
 
is proposed to address a glare concern, and there is a seasonal issue, the owner of 
the solar energy facility, or its assigns, may have an extension of time to address 
the concern up to six (6) months after glare identified as a concern by the 
Department of Technical and Community Services.  
(7) Solar Panels. 
i. 
Solar panels that visually appear to be broken must be replaced or removed within 
forty-five (45) days of identification by the Department of Technical and 
Community Services.  
(8) Signage.  
i. 
Signage is required for each property developed that notifies the public of the 
facility and a means to contact the facility owner for complaints. In the event that 
the contact information is no longer applicable, new signage with up-to-date 
contact information is required.  
j. Decommissioning. 
(1) A decommissioning plan is required to be submitted and approved by the County prior 
to the application for a building permit. Amendments to the plan prior to 
decommissioning must also be approved by the County.  
(2) Decommissioning by the solar energy facility owner or its assigns must begin within 
three (3) months of any of the following conditions unless a plan for its continuing use 
has been provided and approved by the County: 
i. 
If the generating capacity falls below five (5%) percent of the initial generating 
capacity at installation;  
ii. 
The solar energy facility has been damaged to such an extent that the facility will 
not be replaced or repaired. An example of this would be after a natural disaster.  
iii. Upon the abandonment of the facility by the utility scale solar energy facility 
owner or its assigns as determined by the Department of Technical and 
Community Services.  
(3) Decommissioning must be completed within one (1) year once begun unless being 
conducted by the County.  
(4) If a partial decommissioning occurs, a new decommissioning plan and associated cost 
estimates must be prepared.  
(5) Following decommissioning, the Department of Technical and Community Services 
must perform an inspection of the property(s) to determine adequacy of the 
decommissioning and adherence to the decommissioning plan before any financial 
surety will be released.   
(6) Decommissioning plan elements must include, but are not limited to: 
i. 
Decommissioning cost estimates; 
ii. 
Removal of all above ground structures including, but not limited to: solar panel 
arrays, inverters and transformers, concrete pads, internal roads materials, 
fencing, and other debris. Landscaping must be removed, unless a signed written 
statement is provided by the property owner(s) stating otherwise, with the 
exception of land that falls in a Chesapeake Bay Critical Area; 
iii. Removal of underground wiring and other structures; 
iv. A plan for decommissioned material that includes reclamation, salvage, 
recycling, and disposal;   
v. 
Estimates for the amount of materials to be salvaged along with a contingency 
plan in the event that the local salvage market is inundated;

<!-- page 84 -->
Section 7. Overlay and Floating Districts 
Somerset County Zoning Ordinance – February 2024 
7-18 
 
vi. Restoration of the property(s) to a similar or better condition than at the time of 
installation. This may include reseeding, tilling, or reforesting.  
(7) A decommissioning cost estimate must accompany the decommissioning plan and be 
prepared by a Maryland licensed engineer at the cost of the facility owner(s). The 
decommissioning cost estimate must be updated every five (5) years by a Maryland 
licensed engineer at the cost of the facility owner(s) and submitted to the Department of 
Technical and Community Services. Prior to decommissioning the facility, a cost 
estimate must be prepared by a Maryland licensed engineer regardless of the amount of 
time that has passed since the prior cost estimate. The County may prepare its own 
decommissioning cost estimate with the cost to be borne by the owner(s) of the facility.  
(8) If the facility has continued in its use after a period of thirty (30) years, either by ‘re-
racking’ or installing newer systems or technologies, a revised decommissioning plan 
and accompanying financial surety must be submitted to include changes to the facility.  
(9) No more than eighty (80%) percent of the reclamation or salvage value may be used in 
determining a financial surety. Twenty (20%) percent of the cost of decommissioning 
must be provided to the County in a form approved by the County prior to commercial 
operation. At the time of decommissioning, if the reclamation or salvage value exceeds 
the costs to decommission as determined by a Maryland licensed engineer, the financial 
surety will be released once decommissioning has been completed and approved by the 
County. 
 
7.6 Growth Allocation Floating District (GA). Procedures for review and approval of the Growth 
Allocation Floating Zone District are as provided for in the Somerset County Critical Area 
Ordinance.  
 
7.7 Procedure for Floating Zone District Approval.  
a. Application. Application for a floating zone district approval is required to be submitted to the 
Board of County Commissioners for referral to the Planning Commission. Applications must 
include: 
(1) A written petition for location of a floating zone district and approval of a development 
plan, signed by the property(s) owners and contract purchasers, if any, of the property 
that is the subject of the petition.  
(2) A narrative describing the following: 
i. Statement of present and proposed ownership of all land within the development; 
ii. Overall objectives of the proposed development plan and a statement of how the 
proposed development concept corresponds to and complies with the purpose of 
the district; 
iii. The method of providing sewer and water service and other utilities, such as, but 
not limited to: telephone, gas, and electric services, if applicable.  
iv. The method of and responsibility for maintenance of open areas, private streets, 
recreational amenities, and parking areas; 
v. A general description of architectural and landscape elements, including graphic 
representations; and 
vi. If the applicant desires to develop the property in phases, a preliminary phasing 
plan indicating:

<!-- page 85 -->
Section 7. Overlay and Floating Districts 
Somerset County Zoning Ordinance – February 2024 
7-19 
 
(a) The phase(s) in which the project will be developed, including the 
approximate land area, uses, densities, and public facilities to be 
developed during each phase.  
(b) If different land use types are to be included within the development plan, 
the plan must include the mix of uses anticipated to be built in each phase.  
b. Public Hearings. A public hearing shall be held on requests for a floating zone district 
designation by the Planning Commission and Board of County Commissioners, respectively. 
The public hearings must be advertised in accordance with the Land Use Article, Annotated 
Code of Maryland. Adjacent property owners are required to be notified by the applicant via 
certified mail. Certified mail receipts must be received by the Department of Technical and 
Community Services at least five (5) days prior to the public hearing. 
c. Planning Commission Review. The Planning Commission, after holding a public hearing, 
shall make an analysis of and recommendation concerning the proposed floating zone district 
to the County Commissioners. 
d. Board of County Commissioners Decision. The County Commissioners, upon consideration 
of the recommendations of the Planning Commission, may approve a floating zone district 
when it finds that the proposed district satisfies the purposes and standards of this section, 
including the following: 
(1) All necessary infrastructure for the proposed development is or will be made available 
to the district at the time and location required. 
(2) The proposed district will not adversely affect the health, safety, and general welfare of 
residents or workers in the area.  
(3) The proposed district is compatible with and will not be detrimental to the use or 
development of adjacent properties or the general neighborhood. 
(4) The proposed development has been designed so as to minimize possible adverse effects 
on adjacent properties or on the immediate surrounding area.  
(5) The proposed development will not adversely affect the transportation network or unduly 
burden water, sewer, schools, parks, stormwater management areas, including streams 
or other public facilities.  
(6) The proposed development will not adversely affect the environmental or historical 
assets of particular interest to the community as may be identified in any adopted plan 
of the county.  
e.  ‘Change or Mistake’ Rule Does Not Apply. It shall not be necessary for the applicant to show 
a mistake in the original zoning district boundaries or a change in the character of the 
neighborhood in order for the Board of County Commissioners to approve the floating zone 
district request.   
f. Upon final approval by the Board of County Commissioners, the floating zone district 
boundary line shall be shown on the Official Zoning Map.

Dear County Commissioners, 
On behalf of our client, Nexamp, Inc. (Nexamp), Bohler would like to provide formal confirmation and 
acceptance of the Somerset County Planning Commission Hearing scheduled for March 6, 2025, to review 
the Somerset County Utility Scale Solar Energy Facility Floating Zone (SEF) request.  
At this time, our client (Nexamp) seeks Somerset County’s review of the Utility Scale Solar Energy Facility 
Floating Zone (SEF) application to 10960 Kemp Nursery Rd, Princess Anne, MD 21853 (Map: 0022 Grid: 
0006 Parcel: 0284). The subject parcel is currently owned by Kathleen E Keller, with no change in ownership 
proposed. The proposed solar project would include a lease agreement, for a lease area of approximately 
30.00± acres, between Keller and Nexamp for the duration of the solar energy system use. A lease term of 
20-40 years is anticipated to be set in place.
The proposed development of the 5 MWac facility complies with the SEF Zone, generally required to 
generate more than two (2) megawatts in electricity (AC) that are appropriately sited and sized. The Kemps 
Solar Facility project is to be designed as three (3) independent systems, with generation capacities of 2.0 
MWac, 2.0 MWac, and 1.0 MWac, for a combined project total of 5 MWac, and will be placed on 
approximately 30.00± acres of the overall 136.861-acre parcel. The facility intends to connect into the 
Delmarva Power grid and transfer generated clean electricity to the local distribution grid. The development 
is intended to be installed in one phase, with no phasing of the project construction proposed.  
Water, sewer, telecommunications, and gas utility services are not anticipated to be needed for the Kemps 
Solar Facility. Electric service / utility improvements will be limited to the improvements necessary to 
interconnect to the Delmarva Power grid, to provide the 5MWac of electricity anticipated to be generated at 
the Facility.   
Somerset Kemps Solar is to be owned, operated, and maintained by Nexamp, with regular inspections and 
maintenance of open areas, access drives, landscaping, and other site features as required, within the leased 
property area. Nexamp Asset Management Services (NAMS) monitors the system performance remotely 
24/7/365, and additionally will conduct site visits as on a regular basis for preventative, planned, and 
unplanned system inspection & maintenance as determined by the NAMS O&M Plan. Vegetation 
management at site will occur at a frequency to be determined by the NAMS Vegetation Management Plan.  
Once the site is fully operational, site visits are anticipated on average of 6-12 times per year. Parcel lands 
outside the lease area are to remain owned and maintained by current property owner Kathleen E Keller.

To Whom it May Concern: 
We represent Nexamp, Inc. (the “Applicant” or “Nexamp”), on behalf of the landowner, Kathleen 
Keller (the “Property Owner”), with regard to the enclosed Utility Scale Solar Energy Facility 
Floating Zone Application to allow the use of portions of the Property as a Solar Energy Facility 
(the “Facility” or the “Project”). The Applicant has acquired rights (through a ground lease) from 
the Property Owner to develop the Facility on the Property. At this time, our client proposes a solar 
energy facility that generates a combined 5 MWac of electricity. The Project is designed as three (3) 
independent systems, with generation capacities of 2.0 MWac, 2.0 MWac, and 1.0 MWac. The 
proposed Project use requires application of the Utility Scale Solar Energy Facility Floating Zone 
(SEF). The Project will be placed on approximately 30.00± acres of the overall 136.861-acre parcel. 
Enclosed please find a Site Plan in conformance Section 8.15 and 7.5 of the Somerset County Zoning 
Ordinance. A Landscape Plan and photo rendering are also enclosed.  
The Project will connect into the Delmarva Power & Light grid and transfer generated clean 
electricity to the local distribution grid. The Project is intended to be installed in one phase. No 
phasing of the Project construction is currently proposed. The Applicant initially submitted a utility 
interconnection application to Delmarva Power & Light in October 2023. Nexamp amended its 
application in April 2024 and is still awaiting commencement of its interconnection study by the 
utility. Delmarva Power & Light applications are studied sequentially and there remains at least one 
project ahead of Nexamp in the interconnection application queue. The known utility 
interconnection facilities that are required on the Property are depicted on the conceptual site 
plan.  Delmarva Power & Light is responsible to study and provide a detailed scope breakdown of 
the distribution grid improvements and potential substation upgrades that are to be performed by the 
utility (not the Project) in order to enable Project interconnection. 
At this time, Nexamp has no intent to pursue an application for a Certificate of Public Convenience 
and Necessity (“CPCN”) with the Maryland Public Service Commission (“PSC”). It is Nexamp’s 
goal to work with Somerset County staff, the Planning Commission, County Commissioners, and 
the surrounding community to successfully develop and operate this Project. The Project will be 
owned, operated, and maintained by the Nexamp for the duration of the lease term. As the long-term

<!-- page 89 -->
www.BohlerEngineering.com 
Somerset County 
Nexamp – Somerset Kemps 
February 24, 2025 
BEVA # DEA240019.00 
Page 2 of 3 
owner and operator of the Facility, Nexamp wishes to work collaboratively and establish a lasting, 
long-term relationship with Somerset County and the community.  
Nexamp will regularly inspect and maintain the Project, open areas, access drives, landscaping, and 
other site features within the lease area. Such maintenance shall conform to a County approved long 
term maintenance plan pursuant to Section 7.5(e)(2) of the Somerset County Zoning Ordinance. 
Nexamp looks forward to working collaboratively with the County on the long term maintenance 
plan for the Project. Nexamp Asset Management Services (NAMS) monitors the system 
performance remotely 24/7/365, and additionally will conduct site visits as on a regular basis for 
preventative, planned, and unplanned system inspection and maintenance. The long term 
maintenance plan will address all items listed in Section 7.5(e)(2) and Nexamp will provide an 
appropriate surety as required.  
Inverters, transformers, and switchgear will be located in the interior of the site, setback at least 600 
feet from existing dwellings, as per bulk regulation code requirements.  Solar modules (panels) will 
be setback at least 75 feet from property lines. Construction is anticipated to last between 6-9 
months.  The vast majority of construction traffic will be limited to personal vehicles operated by 
contractors and employees working at the site, with only occasional large deliveries of materials by 
tractor-trailer trucks. During operation, planned maintenance inspections and vegetation 
management visits will occur on average 1-2 times per month (during standard business hours). 
Nexamp and Bohler will submit a drainage plan as part of stormwater management in conformance 
with current Maryland regulations.  
As for the remaining 100+/- acres, it is the Applicant’s current understanding that farming will 
continue on the remainder of the agricultural areas of the Property. The Project layout will be 
designed in consideration of continued farming operations and will allow adequate access to farmed 
areas. 
Should you have any questions regarding the enclosed Utility Scale Solar Energy Facility Floating Zone 
Application and Site Plan, please do not hesitate to contact this office at (302) 644-1155.  Thank you.

<!-- page 94 -->
Description
Land capability classification shows, in a general way, the suitability of soils for 
most kinds of field crops. Crops that require special management are excluded. 
The soils are grouped according to their limitations for field crops, the risk of 
damage if they are used for crops, and the way they respond to management. 
The criteria used in grouping the soils do not include major and generally 
expensive landforming that would change slope, depth, or other characteristics of 
the soils, nor do they include possible but unlikely major reclamation projects. 
Capability classification is not a substitute for interpretations that show suitability 
and limitations of groups of soils for rangeland, for woodland, or for engineering 
purposes.
In the capability system, soils are generally grouped at three levels-capability 
class, subclass, and unit. Only class and subclass are included in this data set.
Capability classes, the broadest groups, are designated by the numbers 1 
through 8. The numbers indicate progressively greater limitations and narrower 
choices for practical use. The classes are defined as follows:
Class 1 soils have few limitations that restrict their use.
Class 2 soils have moderate limitations that reduce the choice of plants or that 
require moderate conservation practices.
Class 3 soils have severe limitations that reduce the choice of plants or that 
require special conservation practices, or both.
Class 4 soils have very severe limitations that reduce the choice of plants or that 
require very careful management, or both.
Class 5 soils are subject to little or no erosion but have other limitations, 
impractical to remove, that restrict their use mainly to pasture, rangeland, 
forestland, or wildlife habitat.
Class 6 soils have severe limitations that make them generally unsuitable for 
cultivation and that restrict their use mainly to pasture, rangeland, forestland, or 
wildlife habitat.
Class 7 soils have very severe limitations that make them unsuitable for 
cultivation and that restrict their use mainly to grazing, forestland, or wildlife 
habitat.
Class 8 soils and miscellaneous areas have limitations that preclude commercial 
plant production and that restrict their use to recreational purposes, wildlife 
habitat, watershed, or esthetic purposes.
Rating Options
Aggregation Method: Dominant Condition
Component Percent Cutoff: None Specified
